ParlaMint-HR_2013-04-10-0.u35576	Your Honor, I thank you for asking. Dear MPs, MPs, the intention of the Croatian Government, is primarily the EU Regional Development Ministry of the Fund to adopt a consistent set of measures in the field of regional development policy one of the areas where changes need to be made and to improve the current situation in relation to the legal framework of setting less developed areas. At present, we have several parallel systems of determining assisted areas, from the area of special state concern, from mountain and mountain areas to assisted areas measured by the development index. And right now we have a situation and local units that are truly extremely in deep socioeconomic difficulties and deserve special support from the state, but also in the current system, we have some units that actually don't deserve the help of the state by the standard standards of development. So the government's intention is to adopt a new legal framework that will unify this system, which will standardise the criteria of development where it will have a socio-economic character as the main feature of the degree of development and then we will also coordinate development measures. So the answer is that, a new legislative solution is being prepared where the identification of areas lagging behind will measure exclusively on socioeconomic criteria and, at the same time, we will get an instrument that allows us to better target individual areas with resources from structural areas where, of course, we have a great opportunity in view of the significant resources that the Republic of Croatia has at our disposal.

ParlaMint-HR_2013-04-10-0.u35809	Dear MPs, thank you very much for asking. So, the Labour Act is a law that regulates fundamental social relations in the Republic of Croatia and, as we have already announced, it will be changed in two steps. So, the first change will be very soon in the Parliament. The consultation is under way in the European Commission and we are waiting for a positive opinion from the European Commission, given that part of the Labour Act is in line with the European Workers' Councils, so I hope that we will have a debate in Parliament on the amendments to the Labour Act very quickly. And we have also announced that we are moving towards a full change of working legislation, so I predict that the amendments, or the new Labour Act, will be in our first reading sometime by the end of the summer session of the Parliament. In these changes, which will be very soon ahead of you on benches, I am pleased that we have reached a consensus with the social partners, and we have solved some problems, so other than compliance with the EU, we have solved some of the institutions that are very important for working relations in the Republic of Croatia, so we have made it possible for the changes to be employed for a period of time longer than 3 years if the worker is employed on a fixed time basis of a working contract with the fact that we have made it possible to work longer for a certain period of time, but that must be the reason for the employment. So, whether it's a substitute for someone in maternity, is it a project task, so is it possible, is employment related to some kind of business project, but we have, on the other hand, prevented abuses in the consecutive conclusion of a work contract for a certain time because we have made it clear that it is necessary to place clearly in the following contracts the reason why an additional contract for work is concluded for a certain time. And we have also prevented annexes to this first labour contract from making it impossible for the work to be extended indefinitely for a certain period of time. Also, by prescribing the trial work as a special reason for cancelling the work contract we simplified the Labour Act and prevented the interpretation of the courts who interpreted the trial work as a classic labor contract and there was a problem with the cancellation during the trial period. We have also timed out the procedure for collective redundancies of extra workers, which will result in faster restructuring of companies. At the request of individual trade unions, in agreement with the social partners of the interventions in certain provisions on working time, we have allowed for better quality and efficient work to be arranged, especially in the case of employers working in seasonal activities or employers dealing with the port freighter. So these are all those changes that will be in front of you in benches, but what we expect in the next period will be a very intense effort to bring the new Labour Act into line, especially when we talk about the Labour Act having to answer three questions. One thing is, he has to protect the worker, he's always the one who's weaker. On the other hand, it must enable the employer to be restructured as easily and as quickly as possible. And also, in the Labour Act, we need to solve problems that are seen during court practice, interpretation of individual institutes. I hope that together with the social partners we can reach an agreement and pass a new Labour Act by the end of this year.

And now the question is, should we always have such a bienko trust and should we control how budget funds are spent and what is their effect? So, neither was the effect controlled, nor was it controlled how the funds were spent? If we had made a small sample to see how this was budgetary surveillance 2008-2011 in the Croatian Agency for Small Economy means 309 million kuna was spent, the Control Department did not exist at all and we have analyzed somewhere 321 projects through that control. And unfortunately, the results were devastating. A lack of transparency in the work of the commission was found primarily, unclear criteria for certain entrepreneurs to receive support and others not to receive support. The same number of points used to mean you got 15 thousand kuna and some times 100 or 150, of course without any explanation. Users did not provide evidence of spending, users sometimes delivered fictitious accounts, sometimes when you look at each other's accounts, you see that the signature is always identical. They just copied the accounts. So a lot of irregularities were found, and all of this resulted in no control. And when you spend more than a billion kuna in four years, then you have to control how that money is spent. And we have very quickly established control mechanisms and allocations of funds on two occasions and 5% at each half-yearly support granted directly controlled on the ground. For the first time, ministry employees go out in the field and watch what was invested and whether what was written in the account was purchased. So we're doing all this to justify as effectively as possible the money that's given out of the budget and which isn't small. And you yourself said that it was almost 730 million kuna, though today after the budget rebalances will be somewhat less but it's still much more than it has been in recent years. And very important to add 50% of the funds are provided by entrepreneurs. So it's a direct investment aid that will result in over a billion and 200, 300 million kuna investments this year. So, for the first time in years, we have last year, and you shouldn't always be proud of having made 100% of the budget because it usually means that you haven't even controlled who you gave the money to. My budget was made last year at 97.98% because we still looked at the way money was allocated. For the first time, certain return to the budget was made because we found that certain entrepreneurs did not respect what they took over from the contract. And last year, over 2 million kunas were returned to the budget through the Small Economy Agency and the Ministry of Entrepreneurship, we found a debt of 44 million kuna at the beginning of the mandate and we managed to solve over 35 million kuna in the first year of the term of office. So I can say very responsibly that unfortunately in the past term I'll conclude with this sentence, he didn't control the money spent and the incentives given, and now we're going to use that money very efficiently and productively. ParlaMint-HR_2013-04-10-0.u35882	Thank you very much. On 10 April, the Committee on Human Rights and the Rights of National Minorities discussed the proposal to amend and supplement the national budget of the Republic of Croatia. The Committee considered that proposal through an interested working body. Starting from its section of the Rules of Procedure of the Croatian Parliament, the Committee discussed in more detail those parts of the Proposal for amending and supplementing the state budget relating to the financing of programmes and activities aimed at achieving the rights of national minorities, as well as the protection and promotion of human rights. It has been concluded that budget items directly related to the mentioned areas do not change, i.e. remain earlier, at the earlier planned level. However, it has been stressed that budget items that are significantly reduced by rebalancing are largely related to some of the most vulnerable categories of the population. Thus, funds for the care of exiles, returnees and refugees are reduced by 85 million kuna on the housing item in the areas of special state care, which is a reduction of about 30% and shows that housing programmes, exile and refugee populations continue to be realised in unsatisfactory dynamics. The question has been raised whether, in the given circumstances, the funds should have been rerouted to purchase housing housing outside the area of special public care in order to speed up the resolution of at least that problem. The funds at the position of the Ministry of Health are reduced by 120 million kuna, as it is pointed out in the debate to have direct consequences on the quality of health care, which is also one human right of course, which is still satisfactory, both in terms of adults and in terms of the protection of children's health. In particular, it is pointed out that despite the poor state of investment in the health infrastructure, funds are reduced by 15 million kunas and benefits for the insurance companies by 105 million kuna. It has also been warned that at the position of the most vulnerable categories of the population, there will also be consequences for the fact that the funds provided for for the unemployment benefits are reduced by 55 million kuna in a situation where unemployment is still growing. Finally, after the debate was held, the Committee decided to propose to the Croatian Parliament to adopt amendments and additions to the national budget of the Republic of Croatia in 2013. Thank you very much.
